Python's ``==`` almost does this, but it also equates ``True`` with ``1`` and\n    ``False`` with ``0``, which jq does not; booleans are therefore matched by identity of type\n    before anything else.\n    \"\"\"\n    if isinstance(left, bool) or isinstance(right, bool):\n        return isinstance(left, bool) and isinstance(right, bool) and left is right\n    if isinstance(left, (int, float)) and isinstance(right, (int, float)):\n        return left == right\n    if isinstance(left, list) and isinstance(right, list):\n        return len(left) == len(right) and all(jv_equal(a, b) for a, b in zip(left, right))\n    if isinstance(left, dict) and isinstance(right, dict):\n        return left.keys() == right.keys() and all(jv_equal(left[k], right[k]) for k in left)\n    if type(left) is not type(right):\n        return False\n    return left == right\n\n\ndef _decode(line: str) -> tuple[bool, object]:\n    try:\n        return True, json.loads(line)\n    except ValueError:\n        return False, line\n\n\ndef outputs_match(expected: list[str], actual: list[str]) -> bool:\n    if len(expected) != len(actual):\n        return False\n    for want, got in zip(expected, actual):\n        want_ok, want_value = _decode(want)\n        got_ok, got_value = _decode(got)\n        if want_ok and got_ok:\n            if not jv_equal(want_value, got_value):\n                return False\n        elif want.strip() != got.strip():\n            return False\n    return True\n\"\"\"Precedence-aware jq expression parser.\"\"\"\nimport json\nimport re\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom .ast import Filter, Node, StringTemplate, Format\nfrom .errors import CompileError\nfrom .lexer import tokenize\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass T: text: str\nOPS=(\"//=\",\"?//\",\"!=\",\"==\",\"|=\",\"+=\",\"-=\",\"*=\",\"/=\",\"%=\",\"<=\",\">=\",\"..\",\"//\")\n# jq's query grammar gives the pipe lower precedence than comma.  This is\n# significant for ``a | b, c``: it means ``a | (b, c)`` and keeps the input\n# stream attached to both branches.  The parser's numeric levels increase\n# with binding strength.\nPREC={\"|\":1,\",\":2,\"=\":3,\"|=\":3,\"+=\":3,\"-=\":3,\"*=\":3,\"/=\":3,\"%=\":3,\"//=\":3,\"//\":4,\"or\":5,\"and\":6,\"==\":7,\"!=\":7,\"<\":7,\">\":7,\"<=\":7,\">=\":7,\"+\":8,\"-\":8,\"*\":9,\"/\":9,\"%\":9}\n\ndef lex(s: str) -> list[T]:\n    s=re.sub(r\"#[^\\n]*\",\"\",s);
